堅調なAI関連需要を受け、製造 
業の景況感が改善すると予想

○ 業況判断ＤＩ予測：大企業・製造業   ＋25％Pt（6月調査から3ポイント改善） 
                  大企業・非製造業 ＋37％Pt（6月調査から横ばい） 
○ 2026年度設備投資計画(全規模・全産業)予測：前年度比＋7.8％（6月調査：同＋6.8％）

大企業・製造業の業況判断は
改善、大企業・非製造業の業
況判断は横ばいを予想 
10月1日公表の日銀短観（9月調査）では、大企業・製造業の業況判断DI
が＋25％Ptと、6月調査（＋22％Pt）から3ポイントの改善を予想（図表1）
する。関連指標では、7～9月期法人企業景気予測調査において、大企業の
「貴社の景況判断BSI」は製造業を中心に改善したほか、9月ロイター短観で
は、半導体やデータセンター（DC）関連の受注が好調との声が目立った。AI
関連需要が旺盛ななか、生産用機械や化学、非鉄金属などの関連業種で幅広
く景況感が改善すると見込む。 
図表１ 業況判断DIの予測

### Page 2

2

実際、AI関連財輸出の増勢は拡大している（図表2） 。半導体・電子部品の
輸出は、メモリ価格上昇の恩恵を受けて前年比で大幅プラスとなっているほ
か、足元では半導体製造・検査機器の輸出数量が急増している。その他、DC
に使われる冷却装置などの設備や、半導体製造に不可欠な原材料・素材の輸
出も増加するなど、輸出品目の裾野は広がっているようだ。こうしたもと、
AI関連財の生産指数は2025年9月比＋11.3％と大幅に上昇（図表3）してお
り、AI関連財の増産が鉱工業生産全体のけん引役になっている。 
また、足元にかけて円安基調が継続していることも、こうした輸出志向の
強い製造業種の景況感を下支えしている。 
大企業・非製造業の業況判断DIは＋37％Pt（6月調査：＋37％Pt）と、高
水準が継続する見込みだ。 
既往の資源高を背景に、卸売業の景況感が堅調となることが期待されるほ
か、堅調な投資需要を背景に、建設業でも良好な景況感が続きそうだ。さら
に、不動産価格や賃料の上昇を受けて、不動産業も好調を維持するとみてい
る。 
宿泊・飲食サービス等のインバウンド関連業種の景況感も底堅い結果が予
想される。訪日外客数については、短期的にはやや増勢が鈍化している。中
東情勢悪化を背景とした航空便の減便や、燃油サーチャージの上昇を受けた
航空運賃の負担増などが背景にある。日中対立を受けて中国の訪日外客数が
大幅減となっている状況も不変だ。もっとも、韓国や台湾など近隣諸国から
の訪日外客数は大幅な増加が続いており、全体としてみれば、大幅な腰折れ
は回避されている。 
図表２ 日本のAI関連財輸出額 図表３ 鉱工業生産

### Page 3

3

なお、製造業・非製造業に共通する要因として、中東情勢悪化を受けた石
化製品価格など原材料価格の上昇や人件費上昇といったコスト増による業績
下押し圧力は、日本企業全体としてみれば限定的なものに留まるとみてい
る。大企業やサプライチェーンの上流企業を中心に価格転嫁が進んでおり、
コスト増を一定程度吸収できている。上述の法人企業景気予測調査における
2026年度の経常利益計画が製造業・非製造業ともに上方修正され、増益見込
みに転じていることも、全体としての企業景況感が底堅さを維持するとみて
いる根拠の一つである。ただし、中小企業や、消費者に近いサプライチェー
ンの下流企業では十分な価格転嫁が進まず、景況感の下押し圧力となること
が予想される。 
先行きの業況判断DIは、製造業・非製造業ともに企業規模を問わず悪化が
見込まれる。もっとも、先行きの業況判断DIは現在対比で下振れしやすいク
セのある統計である。中東情勢が引き続き予断を許さないことなど、懸念材
料はあるが、実態としては、日本企業は先行きの景気をさほど悲観的に見て
いない可能性が高い。 
2026年度も、引き続き旺盛な
投資計画が示される見通し 
2026年度の設備投資計画 （全規模 ・ 全産業） は同＋7.8％（6月調査：＋6.8％）
と上方修正を予測する。従来からの旺盛な投資需要に加え、政府が7月以降開
始した「大胆な投資促進税制」に基づく投資支援策も、民間企業の投資需要
を下支えする公算が大きい。中東情勢悪化に伴う不確実性が前回調査時点か
ら幾分後退しているとみられることも、ポジティブな材料だ。 
日本政策投資銀行が公表した 「全国設備投資計画調査（2026年6月）」に よ
れば、化学・電気機械・非鉄金属・電力など、幅広い業種で旺盛なAI関連需
要に対応することを目的とした投資が計画されており、投資のけん引役とな
ることが期待される。 
不動産やサービス業では、ホテル開発や娯楽施設の高付加価値化といった
インバウンド関連投資も好調のようだ。自動車では、EV関連投資が一部減退
し、2025年度計画比では控えめな計画になることが予想されるものの、全体
としてみれば旺盛な投資需要が示されるだろう。

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

SK하이닉스 (000660)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

Document 134: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로삼성전자(005930) 긴 호흡으로긴 호흡으로 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

(005930) 
삼성전자 
2026.09.29

반도체 손인준 
6154 / sij@eugenefn.com

긴 호흡으로 
 투자의견 STRONG BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 560,000원(유지) 
 현재주가 270,000원(9/28)

 삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익이 103.6조원(+16%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 메모리 가격 등 전반적인 반도체 업황에 대한 기존 가정은 대체로 
유지했으나, 최근 원화 강세에 따라 원/달러 환율 가정을 1,440원에서 
1,400원으로 낮추고 MX 사업부의 적자 확대를 반영해 실적 추정치를 하
향 조정했다. 또한 2Q26 재고가 3Q26 매출로 인식되는 과정에서 발생하
는 추가 성과급 충당금 부담도 반영했다.  
 DS 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 104.0조원(+17%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. 서버 DRAM과 eSSD 중심의 가격 인상이 이어지며, DRAM과 
NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +17%, +18%QoQ 상승할 것으로 추정한
다. 1cnm 수율 개선과 HBM4의 빠른 램프업도 실적 개선에 기여할 전망이
다. Foundry/LSI는 HBM4 베이스다이 생산 확대, Groq 3 LPU 양산 및 선
단 공정 가격 인상에 힘입어 하반기 매출 성장이 예상된다. 다만 성과급 충
당금과 감가상각비 부담으로 영업적자 축소 폭은 제한적일 것으로 보인다. 
 DX 부문의 3Q26 영업이익은 -1.8조원(적자지속qoq)을 기록할 것으로 추
정한다. 삼성전자는 메모리 가격 급등에도 제품 가격 인상을 제한하며 시장 
점유율 확대에 주력하고 있다. 이러한 가격 정책과 갤럭시 Z 폴드8 등 신제
품 흥행에 힘입어 판매량은 당초 예상치를 상회할 것으로 보인다. 다만 원
가 상승분의 전가가 제한되면서 영업적자 폭은 크게 확대될 전망이다.  
 삼성전자의 4Q26 영업이익은 114.9조원(+11%qoq)을 기록할 것으로 전
망한다. DRAM과 NAND의 Blended ASP는 각각 +11%, +12%QoQ 상승
하며 견조한 흐름을 이어갈 것으로 보인다. 환율 가정 조정에 따라 2026년
과 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 각각 365.2조원(+735%yoy), 595.8조원
(+63%YoY)으로 낮췄다. 4Q26~1Q27 범용 DRAM·NAND 및 2027년 
HBM 가격 협상 환경은 공급업체에 유리하게 전개되고 있어, 향후 가격 가
정의 추가 상향 가능성이 높다고 판단한다.  
 극심한 DRAM 공급 부족으로 인한 HBM 적층 단수 하향 조정(base die 수
요 증가), Memory Wall에 대응하기 위한 3D Stacked DRAM·zHBM 도입 
논의 등은 파운드리의 기술적 가치를 부각시키는 요인이다. 파운드리의 수
익성 회복 속도는 완만할 전망이지만, 메모리와 파운드리를 연계한 턴키 비
즈니스의 시너지는 AI 반도체 시장 내 경쟁력을 높이고, 메모리 시장 점유
율 확대에도 기여할 것으로 보인다. 적극적인 메모리 가격 인상 정책으로 
업계 최고 수준의 Blended ASP도 장기간 유지할 것으로 예상한다. 
 이제 메모리 업종 주가는 업황의 지속 가능성을 확인하고 꾸준한 주주환원
을 반영하며, 중장기적으로 점진적인 상승세를 이어갈 것으로 판단한다. 삼
성전자는 메모리·파운드리를 아우르는 턴키 역량과 가격 정책에 기반한 실
적 차별화를 통해 피어 그룹 내 경쟁력을 높여갈 것으로 보인다.

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에도 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

Document 157: AI重塑中国股市格局！高盛详解中国AI投资双主线

Source PDF: all report pdf/AI重塑中国股市格局！高盛详解中国AI投资双主线.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 6
Status: ok

### Page 1

⾼盛最新报告揭⽰， DeepSeek 横空出世以来，中国 AI 股票已创造近 5 万亿美元市值，占 A
股和 H 股成交额⾼达 57% 。⾼盛将投资机会锁定为 " 出⼝商 " 与 " 内需⻰头 " 两条主线 —— 前者
累计涨幅达 126% ，后者在近期回调中更具韧性。更关键的是，国际投资者对中国 AI 股票配
置⽐例仅 5.8% ，显著低配或意味着巨⼤资⾦流⼊空间。
摘要：
AI 浪潮正在从根本上改变中国股票市场的结构与投资逻辑。
据追⻛交易台，⾼盛最新研究报告显⽰，⾃ 2025 年初 DeepSeek 时刻以来，中国 AI 相关上市公司
已通过股价上涨和 IPO 合计创造近 5 万亿美元市值， AI 股票⽬前占 A 股和 H 股总市值的 42% 、成交
额的 57% ，成为主导中国资本市场的核⼼叙事。
⾼盛在报告中将中国 AI 投资机会明确划分为两条主线：⼀是出⼝导向型 AI 企业，作为西⽅ AI 资
本开⽀热潮的受益代理；⼆是内需驱动型国内 AI ⻰头，受益于中国本⼟ AI ⽣态建设与政策强⼒

⽀持。⾼盛认为，尽管地缘政治与贸易政策⻛险犹存，两条主线均具备可观的投资价值。
在市场影响层⾯，⾼盛数据显⽰，中国 AI 出⼝商⾃ 2025 年初⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉累计回报达 126% ，但
⾃ 6 ⽉底全球 AI 股回调以来已从⾼点下跌 23% ；⽽国内 AI 企业在此轮调整中表现出更强韧性，相
对跑赢约 10 个百分点。⾼盛同步更新了包含 50 只个股的 " 中国精选 AI 组合 " ，该组合⾃ 2025 年 2 ⽉
成⽴以来累计上涨 33% ，夏普⽐率达 2.23 ，显著优于 MSCI 中国指数的 0.74 。
AI 重塑中国股市：近 5 万亿美元市值凭空⽽⽣
DeepSeek 的横空出世标志着中国 AI 投资叙事的历史性转折点。据⾼盛统计，⾃ 2025 年初⾄今，
中国 AI 相关上市公司市值增量接近 5 万亿美元，其中股价上涨贡献约 2.8 万亿美元， IPO 贡献约 2
万亿美元。
从市场结构看， AI 主题对中国股市的渗透程度已⼤幅提升。AI 股票占全部 A 股和 H 股市值的⽐例

从 2024 年的约 20% 升⾄当前的 42% ，在成交额中的占⽐更⾼达 57% ， IPO 融资额占⽐亦从 2024
年的 4% 跃升⾄ 2026 年迄今的 13% 。
从盈利基本⾯看，中国 AI 企业与⾮ AI 企业的分化同样显著。⾼盛数据显⽰，中国 AI 企业 2023 年
⾄ 2028 年预期每股盈利复合增速为 16% ，⽽⾮ AI 企业仅为 4% 。在全球 AI 股权宇宙中，中国 AI 企
业市值约为 7.6 万亿美元（按 AI 业务敞⼝加权），占全球 AI 市值约 10% ，占全球 AI 收⼊约 24% ，占
资本开⽀约 14% 。
中美 AI 双极格局成形：中国补强内循环⽣态
⾼盛报告指出，全球 AI 双极格局的叙事正在投资者中获得⼴泛认同 —— 以美国为核⼼的 AI ⽣态系
统与以中国为核⼼的 AI ⽣态系统并⾏发展，背后驱动⼒涵盖地缘政治、国家安全与经济增⻓等多
重维度。
中国在全球 AI 供应链中已占据举⾜轻重的地位。在稀⼟精炼、电⼒设备、 PCB 、光模块及成熟
制程半导体等细分领域，中国的全球市场份额均超过 50% 。与此同时，中国正逐步构建⼀套内

循环、⽇趋⾃给⾃⾜的 AI 价值链，在先进封装、开源⼤语⾔模型（ LLM ）、算⼒⽣产与消费，以
及物理 AI 应⽤等领域取得突破。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

从财务数据看，中国 AI 企业整体市值为 7.6 万亿美元， 2026 年预期净利润率为 12% 、 ROE 为
16% ；⽽全球其他地区 AI 企业对应数据分别为 55.1 万亿美元、 24% 和 29% ，差距依然悬殊。
两条主线分化：出⼝商与内需⻰头各有逻辑
⾼盛将中国 AI 股权宇宙（市值超 1 亿美元的标的逾 1700 家）按收⼊来源地域划分为两⼤阵营，两
者在驱动因素、⻛险敞⼝和市场表现上均呈现明显分化。
出⼝导向型 AI 企业：⾼盛识别出 360 余家上市公司，合计市值约 1.7 万亿美元，其海外收⼊占⽐
超过 40% 。这类企业主要集中于 AI 基础设施和电⼒设备领域，被投资者视为西⽅ AI 资本开⽀热潮
的受益代理。从历史表现看，该群体⾃ 2025 年初⾄ 2026 年 6 ⽉累计回报达 126% ，领先国内 AI 企
业 84 个百分点，主要得益于更强劲的盈利兑现和增⻓预期。但⾃ 6 ⽉底全球 AI 股回调以来，该群
体已从⾼点下跌 23% ，相对优势收窄约 10 个百分点。⾼盛分析显⽰，出⼝型 AI 企业对全球 AI 股

权⻉塔及中美关系晴⾬表的敏感度更⾼。
国内 AI ⻰头企业：⾼盛识别出 880 余家主要服务国内市场的上市公司（海外收⼊占⽐低于
10% ），合计市值约 3.2 万亿美元。这⼀群体受益于中国超⼤规模云服务商 AI 资本开⽀的快速增
⻓、 AI 应⽤需求的爆发，以及政策层⾯对科技⾃⽴⾃强的强⼒⽀持。相较于出⼝型企业，国内 AI
⻰头对中国 AI 资本开⽀趋势和国内 AI 政策动向更为敏感，在近期全球 AI 股回调中表现出更强的防
御性，对国际投资者⽽⾔具备⼀定的分散化价值。
主线⼀：中国 AI 出⼝领袖 —— 成⻓与估值的平衡
⾼盛在承认地缘政治与贸易政策⻛险的前提下，仍对中国 AI 出⼝商保持战略性乐观，认为其有望
凭借成本竞争⼒、制造能⼒、研发强度及全球化执⾏⼒进⼀步扩⼤全球市场份额。
在筛选标准上，⾼盛重点关注以下特征：全球市场份额已较⾼（被替代⻛险低）、海外收⼊加速
增⻓（市场份额持续扩张）、技术护城河较深（研发强度⾼于同业）、资本开⽀纪律严格（⾃由
现⾦流为正），以及对地缘政治不确定性敏感度相对较低。

据此筛选出的 " 中国 AI 出⼝领袖 " 名单包含 20 只⾼盛买⼊ / 中性评级标的，覆盖 12 个 AI 相关⾏业，
涉及光模块、⼈形机器⼈、⾃动驾驶、医疗健康、消费硬件、数据中⼼、存储、 PCB/CCL 等领
域。该组合平均 66% 的 2026 年上半年收⼊来⾃海外市场，当前交易于 2027 年预期市盈率 21 倍，
卖⽅⼀致预期未来两年每股盈利复合增速为 38% 。⾼盛指出，该组合估值已回落⾄近年区间低
端，当前 12 个⽉远期市盈率约为 16.5 倍。
主线⼆：国内 AI 冠军 —— 政策红利与稀缺价值
发展 AI 已上升为国家政策⽬标。⾼盛报告梳理显⽰，政策⽀持覆盖供给侧（政府主导投资）、需
求侧（消费推⼴措施）和资本市场（ IPO 绿⾊通道、市场流动性改善）三个维度，⼒度之⼴被认
为超越以往历次⾏业性宽松周期。具体举措包括： 2026 年超⻓期特别国债中约 8000 亿元⼈⺠币
定向⽀持 " 两重 " 领域（第⼆批次明确纳⼊ AI ）；国家⼤基⾦三期规模 3440 亿元⼈⺠币，聚焦先进

封装、设备及 AI 芯⽚；国家 AI 产业投资基⾦规模 600 亿元⼈⺠币；以及第⼗五个五年规划明确要
求研发⽀出年增速超 7% 、到 2030 年达 GDP 的约 3% 。
⾼盛预测，中国 AI 相关 21 个⾏业的总可寻址市场规模到 2030 年有望达到 3 万亿⾄ 4 万亿美元，对
应 2026 年⾄ 2030 年复合增速约 28% 。
" 国内 AI 冠军 " 名单同样包含 20 只标的，覆盖 11 个 AI 相关⾏业，硬科技（半导体设备、 IC 设计、存
储、晶圆代⼯）与软科技（娱乐媒体、电商、⼤模型）各占约半壁江⼭。该组合平均逾 90% 的
收⼊来⾃国内市场，当前交易于 23 倍远期市盈率，对应 33% 的每股盈利复合增速， PEG 约为 0.7

### Page 3

倍。⾼盛提⽰，部分国内半导体本⼟化标的因政策溢价较⾼，估值已显著⾼于全球同业，过度
追⾼政策确定性是该主线的主要⻛险。
组合策略： 50 只个股覆盖 AI 全价值链
在上述两条主线之外，⾼盛同步更新了 " 中国精选 AI 组合 " ，新增及剔除各 15 只个股，⽬前共包含
50 只标的（ 38 只 A 股、 27 只 H 股及 3 只 ADR ，含双重上市股票），覆盖 23 个具体 AI ⾏业，旨在为
投资者提供横跨整条 AI 价值链的均衡敞⼝。
从结构看，该组合保留了对上游板块（电⼒、半导体、基础设施）的较⾼配置，同时纳⼊近期
上市的 AI ⼤模型公司。按⾏业层级划分，半导体占⽐ 35.9% ， AI 应⽤占⽐ 33.6% ，基础设施占⽐
21.2% ，电⼒占⽐ 8.2% ， AI ⼤模型占⽐ 1.2% 。
从财务指标看，该组合（等权重）当前交易于 31 倍 12 个⽉远期市盈率，⼀致预期未来两年盈利
增速为 46% ， 58% 的收⼊直接与 AI 相关业务挂钩。⾃ 2025 年 2 ⽉成⽴以来，该组合累计上涨
33% ，同期 MSCI 中国指数上涨 6% ，夏普⽐率达 2.23 ，远⾼于基准的 0.74 。
⾼盛同时指出，国际投资者对中国 AI 股票的配置仍明显偏低 —— 中国 AI 企业占全球 AI 市值约
10% ，但共同基⾦对中国科技股的实际配置⽐例仅约 5.8% ，低配幅度显著，这或为后续资⾦流
⼊提供潜在空间。

Portfolio Considerations
We view recent volatility as a rolling
correction rather than the end of
the cycle. We favor diversified Equity

exposure, with potential opportunities
extending beyond Growth to Value,
Small- and Mid-caps, Non-U.S. Equities
(especially Emerging Markets), dividend-
focused and thematic strategies.
We favor adjusting portfolios toward
strategic durations given attractive
elevated nominal and real yields, and see
value in high-quality bonds, particularly
longer-dated municipal bonds.
Consider broader portfolio
diversification with potential
opportunities across industrial metals,

energy, Real/Tangible Assets, Hedge
Funds, private markets and global
strategies to capture themes from AI,
reindustrialization, infrastructure growth
and market dispersion.
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Yen, 10-Year Yield and Iran:  Bessent’s Three-Front War and the Stakes for Investors
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Scott Bessent may carry the title of Treasury Secretary, but these days he looks more like
America’s financial field marshal. His battlefield stretches from Tokyo, where he is trying
to engineer a stronger Japan yen; to the U.S. Treasury market, where he is battling to keep

the 10-year yield from breaking decisively above 5%; and Tehran, where he has become the
point man in the escalating U.S. campaign to squeeze Iran financially and economically.
Make no mistake about it: Secretary Bessent and the Treasury Department are increasingly
an instrument of American economic statecraft, charged not simply with financing the
government and overseeing the financial system, but with managing markets, currencies
and geopolitical adversaries simultaneously. Welcome to the new age of financial warfare.

Front One: Defending the Yen. The first front unfolded over the summer when Secretary
Bessent and the BoJ stealthily intervened in support of the yen, which had fallen to a near
40-year low against the dollar by late July. To arrest the yen’s decline, the U.S. Treasury sold
euros instead of dollars in support of the yen, while pushing the BoJ to make use of the
Fed’s emergency repo facilities. In further support of the yen, the BoJ did what Bessent and

the markets have been pining for, raising its official rate by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%.
Since intervening in the foreign exchange markets, the yen has appreciated by nearly 5%
against the U.S. dollar, alleviating concerns of a disorderly unwind of the yen and giving
Bessent—thus far—an important victory. Defending the yen, of course, isn’t simply about
Japan—it’s about the U.S. and the need for foreign capital inflows.
Japan is one of America’s largest foreign creditors. A persistently weak yen creates

pressure on Tokyo to defend its currency, potentially by selling some of its enormous
holdings of foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys. That prospect didn’t sit well with the
Treasury secretary. At precisely the moment Washington needs foreign capital to finance
its enormous fiscal deficits, the last thing Mr. Bessent wants is one of America’s largest
creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities. Hence, the battle to backstop

Japan’s currency.
Front Two: Defending the 10-Year Bond. Mr. Bessent has had less success on the
second front: the U.S. bond market. While the 10-year Treasury yield slipped slightly
following last week’s decision by the Fed to boost the fed funds rate by 25 bps, the
benchmark interest rate is up roughly 85 bps since the start of the year. Stubbornly high
inflation, elevated oil prices, enormous government borrowing requirements and growing
investor anxiety over America’s fiscal trajectory have all converged and conspired to keep

the 10-year yield closer to 5% than 4%.
Field Marshal Bessent is very aware that the 10-year Treasury is the gravitational center of
global finance—and that mortgage rates, corporate borrowing costs, equity valuations and
the government’s own interest bill all take their cue from it. A sustained move materially
above 5% would tighten financial conditions across the American economy, potentially
threaten the Equity bull market, and pile more pressure on the Republican Party.

To prevent this from happening, the Treasury has expanded its bond-buyback operations,
including purchases of longer-dated government securities, in an effort to improve liquidity
and relieve some pressure on the market, while shifting new government debt issuance
into short-term bills. Yet the bond vigilantes have so far proved harder to intimidate than
currency traders. Even after Treasury announced additional purchases, yields continued
climbing before receiving some relief from the latest hawkish move from the Fed.

Over the medium term, if investors believe Washington is borrowing too much, inflation is
running too hot or the supply of bonds is overwhelming global demand, they will demand a
higher yield to own America’s debt. This battle is far from over.
Front Three: Squeezing Tehran. Then there is Iran, where Mr. Bessent is wielding an
entirely different weapon: America’s control over the plumbing of global finance. In practice
Investment Implications
We expect continued investor

support and preference for real hard
assets, and a bias towards a plethora
of commodities and industrial plays.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 21, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
this means the Treasury can make a foreign bank choose between doing business
with Iran or the U.S. It can freeze assets, blacklist companies, restrict transactions and
pressure allies to close financial escape routes.
Under “Operation Economic Outcast,” billed as economic “D-Day,” Treasury has
intensified sanctions against Iranian entities and financial networks, including recent

actions against Iran aviation and additional efforts aimed at banks and organizations
facilitating Tehran’s international transactions. Mr. Bessent has described the objective
in unusually stark terms: to “economically asphyxiate” the Iran regime. This is economic
warfare in its purest form, but whether it works remains to be seen.
“Asphyxiating” an economy takes time and patience—and international cooperation
and coordination. It means closing multiple global loopholes of third-party entities still

willing and able to do business with Iran—look no further than Russian banks. In addition,
squeezing a major oil producer runs the risk of aggravating global oil supplies, keeping
oil prices elevated. Higher energy costs, in turn, feed right back into the U.S. inflation
outlook the Fed is trying to tame and keeps the bond vigilantes on guard and ready to
push back. Not unlike the battle to suppress U.S. bond yields, the battle to bring Iran to
heel is going to be a long one.

How does it all end? How Scott Bessent’s three-front war plays out remains to be seen. A
decisive victory on all three fronts is unlikely since corralling currency and bond markets
is next to impossible, while the whims of geopolitics can lay waste to the best-laid plans.
That said, however, the U.S. Treasury Secretary has signaled that he is ready to take
a very proactive, pro-growth approach to his job. That’s the good news. The less
encouraging news stems from the inconsistencies in the overarching objectives of the

Treasury secretary.
Mr. Bessent can pressure Iran because access to the dollar-based financial system
remains extraordinarily valuable. He can intervene in foreign-exchange markets because
America’s financial resources give Washington enormous clout. And he can attempt to
influence the Treasury market because U.S. government securities remain the foundation
of the global financial system. But therein lies the contradiction.
The U.S. is simultaneously weaponizing the financial system abroad while becoming more

dependent on that same financial system at home. Washington wants the world to fear
losing access to dollars while also asking the world to lend it trillions of dollars. It wants to
use Treasury and dollar dominance as instruments of geopolitical coercion while running
fiscal deficits that require foreigners to continue believing deeply in those very assets.
The more Washington borrows, the more sensitive it becomes to bond-market discipline.
The more aggressively it weaponizes access to the dollar, the greater the incentive for

adversaries to seek alternatives. And the more the Treasury secretary intervenes directly
in markets, the more investors may begin wondering where economic policy ends and
market management begins.
Exhibit 1: Scott Bessent's Three-Front War.
The Front The Problem Bessent's Objective The Weapon
The Yen Weak yen/currency instability Stronger yen Foreign Exchange intervention, policy coordination, pressure on Japan
The Yield Rising 10-year Treasury yield Lower/contained yields Debt-management strategy, buybacks, issuance policy

Iran Tehran's access to global finance Maximum financial pressure Sanctions, asset restrictions, dollar-system leverage
Source: Chief Investment Office.
For the moment, Mr. Bessent is fighting on three fronts. He wants a stronger yen, a
lower 10-year Treasury yield and an economically isolated Iran. Success on any one front
would be difficult. Achieving all three simultaneously will require considerable skill—and
perhaps a little luck.

MARKET VIEW
Beyond AI’s Resource Footprint, Consider its “Handprint”
Anna Potts, CFA, Vice President and Investment Strategist
Since the launch of ChatGPT, investors have celebrated AI’s potential while growing
more concerned about its resource footprint: Behind every AI-generated response is
a physical network of data centers, power plants, transmission lines, cooling systems,

semiconductor facilities and critical-mineral supply chains. No doubt, the AI revolution
has environmental costs. As adoption accelerates, growing demand for power, water,
advanced hardware and raw materials could strain grids, raise emissions and intensify
pressure on local resources. Yet the sustainability conversation should extend beyond
AI’s footprint to consider whether its environmental benefits can outweigh its
resource demands.
AI’s footprint is large, growing and certainly not trivial. AI increases electricity demand,

adds large, concentrated loads to already constrained grids, and may slow the retirement
of polluting energy assets. U.S. data centers could account for 20% of national electricity
consumption by 2035, up from 5.9% today, with power needs reaching 194 gigawatts
(comparable to the electricity demand of more than 150 million homes). 1 Cooling, power
generation and semiconductor manufacturing require water; each new megawatt also
embeds substantial copper, aluminum and other materials; and short hardware cycles

can increase electronic waste. Use varies sharply by workload: Complex reasoning and
agentic tasks can consume many times more energy than a simple text prompt. The
effects are already reaching consumers and communities. Wholesale power costs on
PJM—the largest U.S. grid—rose 76% year-over-year in Q1 2026, while data-center
load included in its previous two capacity auctions added an estimated $13.8 billion to
customer bills. 2 Communities are mobilizing around their concerns, including affordability,

water use and air pollution: Q1 2026 produced the largest single quarterly concentration
of delayed or blocked data-center projects on record. 3 Opposition is therefore becoming
a development constraint to be considered.
Context, caveats and comparisons. Headlines often tell only part of the story. Globally,
data centers account for about 1% of electricity use, 4 modest relative to many industries,
and 9% of estimated global 2025 to 2030 electricity growth. 5 A typical text prompt on

one major company’s AI model 6 uses 0.24 watt-hours of electricity, emits 0.03 grams
of CO₂ and consumes 0.26 milliliters of water—roughly 617,000 prompts match the
emissions from producing and cooking one sirloin steak, while 262,000 match the water
used in an eight-minute shower. 7 Still, billions of interactions, more complex workloads
and growth in resource-constrained regions can create meaningful cumulative impacts.
The most meaningful assessment therefore depends on workload, utilization, location,

grid mix, cooling design, local water availability and who bears the cost of supporting
infrastructure, not one universal number.
AI’s Handprint. While much remains to be seen, AI’s environmental “handprint”—the
benefits it can enable—could potentially prove larger than its direct footprint in some
areas. Across the energy system, AI can help utilities better match electricity supply
and demand, balance grids, optimize storage and reduce wasted energy. Data centers

already account for more than 30% of corporate renewable power-purchase agreements,
helping create long-dated demand for clean generation. 8 Beyond power, AI can improve
precision agriculture, streamline transportation and inventories, and support product

Investment Implications
Responsible AI growth requires
investment in power generation,

grid modernization, energy storage,
advanced cooling, water treatment
and reuse, efficient computing, and
materials recovery and recycling.
Diversified environmental and thematic
strategies may offer exposure to
these physical enablers while reducing
reliance on benchmark names.

design, predictive maintenance, waste sorting and recycling. AI is transforming climate
risk management by using satellite imagery, weather data and predictive analytics to
forecast floods, wildfires and extreme weather events, enabling governments, companies,
and investors to anticipate risks and allocate capital more effectively (Exhibit 2). These
gains are not automatic: they depend on innovation, adoption, and whether efficiency

improvements reduce total resource use rather than simply stimulate more demand. All
industries are faced with the opportunity to responsibly use AI to make operations more
efficient, and much is yet to be discovered.

AI’s potential handprint becomes more tangible in transportation, agriculture and climate
resilience. In aviation, for example, AI can combine weather forecasts, flight paths and
atmospheric conditions to reroute aircraft around regions where warming contrails
are most likely to form—reducing climate impact without requiring a new aircraft or

fuel system. On farms, machine learning can interpret aerial imagery and field-level
sensor data to identify crop stress early, directing water, fertilizer and chemicals to
where they are needed and helping growers improve yields while reducing inputs. AI
can also strengthen climate preparedness by analyzing satellite imagery, weather data
and infrastructure exposure to detect deforestation, anticipate floods or wildfires,
and identify communities, facilities and supply chains most vulnerable to physical

hazards. These are just a few examples that illustrate how AI’s value can extend beyond
incremental efficiency gains to reshape real-world decisions.
Investment Considerations. The challenges created by AI also create potential
investment opportunities. Companies are investing in efficient chips, closed-loop cooling,
reclaimed-water systems, energy storage, grid upgrades and cleaner firm power to
reduce resource intensity and improve reliability. Professionally managed environmental

and thematic strategies can target these “picks and shovels” of the digital buildout,
alongside businesses using AI to improve efficiency across energy management,
water infrastructure, environmental services, waste management and compute. A
disciplined thematic approach can diversify exposure beyond the largest benchmark
constituents while positioning for structural growth in power demand, grid modernization
and resource efficiency. Focusing solely on AI’s footprint risks overlooking both the

companies helping it scale more efficiently and the broader environmental benefits its
handprint may enable.

THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
The Beauty of September
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Call it an eventful first 13 trading days of September: Brent crude re-eclipsed $100 per
barrel, diesel prices surpassed $6 per gallon for the first time in history, the 10-year U.S.
Treasury yield breached 5%, 9 and the Fed joined the ECB and the BoJ in hiking rates for

the first time since July 2023. Through it all, the S&P 500 has shed 1.4% since the start
of the month, while its equal-weighted counterpart is down by 3.3%.
It’s not all too unexpected that September would be a choppy month for U.S. Equities.
Historical seasonal patterns suggest as much. Since 1928, the average S&P 500 return
in September has come out to -1.1%, 10 not only the worst month of the year on average
but also the only month when the benchmark has ended lower more often than it’s

ended higher. Similar weakness has been seen in midterm election years, with the S&P
500 producing an average September return of -0.8% since 1950. 10
The good news is that this has historically been an attractive entry point for U.S. Equity
investors. Call it the beauty of September: In contrast to September’s average decline,
average returns improve in October (+3.0%), November (+2.7%), and December (+0.8%).
Indeed, the S&P 500 has never been negative in the six or 12 months post-midterm

election since 1950 (Exhibit 4A), boasting an impressive average return of 13.8% and
15.8% over those respective periods. 10
Looking ahead to the coming weeks, we have little reason to believe that 2026
will diverge from the historical trend. Volatility is likely to rise into October before
moderating post-election, in line with what’s typical in the months surrounding the
midterms (Exhibit 4B). A quieter corporate calendar ahead of Q3 earnings means

investor focus will remain on incoming macro data, the path of energy prices, and the
Fed’s policy path.
In the meantime, while the S&P 500 still stands just 2.7% below its mid-August high,
churn beneath the surface already appears to be taking place. Just 38% of S&P 500
members now stand above their 50-day moving average versus 70% a mere month ago. 9
Far from worrisome, a correction in the near term in the context of a strong fundamental
economic backdrop would be a potential buying opportunity in our view.

a strong earnings backdrop, we

remain overweight U.S. Equities and
view possible episodic volatility and
weakness in the coming months

in portfolios.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

MACRO STRATEGY
The Five Shocks of 2026—And the Extraordinary Resilience of the U.S. Economy
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
This is the year the consensus got mugged.
Rewind to the start of the year. The market consensus was that the U.S. economy was
set to slow. The Fed was set to cut interest rates. Corporate earnings were set to grow—

but not explode. Energy prices would remain tame. And the odds looked good that the
Republicans would control the House, the Senate, or both, following the midterm election.
Nine months on, the landscape looks starkly different. Defined by five shocks—energy,
interest rates, earnings, growth, and politics—2026 looks nothing like the year investors
thought they were buying on January 1.
Shock 1: Diesel Matters. While global oil prices have moved higher since the start of

the U.S./Iran war on February 28, oil prices have yet to surpass their previous record high
because the world has released some 400 million barrels from strategic reserves this year,
China has curbed its demand for oil imports, and the U.S. has goosed its energy exports to
record highs.
But here is the problem: While gasoline powers the American consumer, diesel powers the
American economy—and diesel prices are now sitting at record highs. Diesel supplies have

been hammered by wars in the Middle East and Ukraine that have sharply reduced refining
capacity. According to the International Energy Agency, by August, exports of diesel from
refiners in the Middle East had fallen by a quarter from prewar levels. Meanwhile, Ukraine
has hit 24 of Russia’s 34 largest refineries, reducing supplies from one of the world’s
largest diesel exporters. The upshot: U.S. diesel prices have soared from $3.55 a gallon
at the start of the year to roughly $6.52 a gallon. While crude oil trades for around $100

a barrel, the retail equivalent of a barrel of diesel now costs more than $260. The diesel
shock of 2026 was the last thing investors saw coming—and remains a key impediment to
structurally lower inflation.
Shock 2: From Interest Rate Cuts to Rate Hikes. Perhaps no other consensus view
entering 2026 has been turned more dramatically on its head than expectations for the
Fed. Heading into 2026, markets were pricing in rate cuts on expectations of a cooling

labor market, moderating inflation, and slower economic growth. The Fed easing cycle was
expected to continue in 2026. In January 2026, futures showed a roughly 50% chance of a
cut by March.
Expectations, however, flipped in late February with the start of the U.S./Iran war. The
Middle East energy shock reignited inflationary pressures, which, combined with stronger-
than-expected U.S. economic growth and a tightening labor market, have demolished the

odds of easier money in 2026. Short- and long-term Treasury yields rose sharply last week
in the wake of the S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index (PMI)
which pointed to the strongest business expansion in more than five years and the fastest
employment growth in over four years, while also reinforcing expectations that the Fed
will maintain a restrictive policy stance for longer. Markets were expecting two to three
quarter-point rate cuts in 2026; instead, the Fed has already raised rates once this year and

is expected to keep raising rates over the near term.
Shock 3: The Earnings Boom No One Saw Coming. Not all the shocks of 2026 are
negative. Indeed, stronger-than-expected corporate earnings have been among the most
positive factors of this year. Corporate America entered the year facing questions about
tariffs, AI-related capital expenditure spending, the health of the U.S. consumer, operating
leverage and the U.S. dollar outlook. At the start of the year, Wall Street was expecting

roughly $290 of S&P 500 earnings per share for 2026, per Bloomberg estimates. But by
September 21, the estimate had risen by $50, to $340 per share.
Investment Implications
While some of these shocks may
contribute to periods of market
volatility, the combination of strong
earnings growth, robust domestic
demand, and ongoing capital
investment suggests that the
fundamental backdrop for Equities
remains constructive.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Earnings have been upgraded and underpinned by several factors, including robust final 
domestic demand in the U.S., a pickup in global growth, AI-related capital spending, 
productivity gains, and enhanced operating leverage. All these variables have helped deliver 
an earnings “shock” to Wall Street. And as an aside, yes, headline profit growth has been 
inflated by strong technology earnings and one-off investment gains from Amazon and 
Alphabet. That said, the S&P 500’s earnings story remains extraordinary. According to 
FactSet, the 493 companies excluding the Magnificent 7 1 
posted 31.8% earnings growth in 
Q2, the strongest level since late 2021.

Shock 4: An Economy with more Horsepower than the Headlines Suggest. That 
corporate profits have exceeded expectations this ye
ar is due in no small part to an economy 
that continues to surprise to the upside. The Fed was supposed to cut rates this year to 
keep the economy humming and prevent it from losing steam. But rather than limp through 
2026, the economy has leaped ahead. Yes, real gross domestic product (GDP) growth of 
2.1% in Q1 and 1.5% in Q2 are hardly blockbuster annualized rates of growth. However, the 
figures belie the underlying strength of the U.S. economy, with real final domestic demand

(which strips out trade) running at a 4.2% annual rate in Q2. According to the latest reading 
of the Atlanta Fed GDP tracker, Q3 real GDP grow
th is running at a red-hot rate of 5.1%. 
Slowdown? What slowdown?
Resilient consumer spending, robust capital investment, record leve
ls of new business 
formation, and strong foreign direct investment inflows have combined to stoke growth 
in the $32 trillion behemoth called the U.S. economy—the most resilient and dynamic 
economy in the world.
Shock 5: The Unexpected Washington Reset. The last shock is still in the making, but 
given current trends, the odds are rising that the Democrats could control both the House 
and Senate follow
ing the midterm elections. To this point, in January, and per Kalshi, the 
Democrats had a 71% shot at the House and an 18% chance of taking the Senate. Fast-
forward to today, and the odds are a 90% chance that the Democrats take the House and a 
60% chance that they take the Senate.
How all of this plays out remains to be seen—there are some 36 days before the November 
vote, and anything can happen. The market base case remains that the Democrats take the 
House, and the Senate is a toss-up—or too close to call. But with the Democrats expected to 
control at least one chamber of Congress, inv
estors can expect more volatility and uncertainty 
about negotiations ov
er the budget, debt ceiling, defense spending and AI regulation. Another 
political wildcard to watch: the changing dynamics within the Democratic Party.
The Biggest Shock of All for 2026? Pe
rhaps it’s not $6 diesel, rising interest rates, 
solid growth, booming profits or shifting politics. Rather, it’s the ability of the U.S. 
economy to absorb the unexpected, not buckle under stress, and continue generating  wealth 
for investors. To wit, the S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth
 consecutive year of double-digit 
returns.

MARKET VIEW
2026 Has, So Far, Defied the Traditional Midterm Year Playbook
Jordy Fuentes, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Current Landscape. To no surprise, market volatility persisted in 2026. Performance initially
tracked historical midterm election patterns before diverging meaningfully as the year
progressed, though there are still three months to go. Several items remain at hand as U.S.

voters head to the polls in November, including geopolitical and foreign policy uncertainty,
energy driven inflation pressures, affordability concerns, U.S. fiscal trajectory, and rapid
advancements in AI. Regardless of the eventual political outcome, history suggests that
markets and the economy can perform well across different configurations of government.
In our view, markets continue to be anchored by strong fundamentals and encouraging
seasonal trends.

To Be Addressed. U.S. domestic and foreign policy remain in focus as the global reorder,
tit-for-tat tariffs, and geopolitical conflicts spanning the Middle East to Eastern Europe have
added layers of uncertainty to the U.S. economic outlook.
At the same time, persistent geopolitical flare-ups have pushed oil prices materially
higher, adding to inflation pressures and keeping them above the Fed’s 2% target.
Headline Consumer Price Index (CPI) held above 3% year-over-year (YoY) in August, while

its core measure, which excludes food and energy, held at 2.4% YoY. The latest Personal
Consumption Expenditure (PCE), the Fed’s preferred measure of inflation, also remained
above 3% YoY for both its headline and core figures. 2
The U.S. fiscal trajectory is another area of focus. The U.S. budget deficit stands at 5.4% of
GDP , just above this century’s average of 4.6%. Meanwhile, U.S. public sector debt surpassed
the $40 trillion mark for the first time in August, oscillating around 100% of GDP .3 While these

trends warrant close monitoring, we view them as a slow-moving structural headwind rather
than an immediate concern, particularly within a highly innovative and diverse U.S. economy.
AI has emerged as a policy consideration, with debates increasingly centered on both
regulatory safeguards and the economic implications of the expanding infrastructure buildout.
For voters, those concerns extend to affordability, as the significant energy requirements of

new data centers raise questions about potential pressure on electricity costs.
Strap In for The Ride. Leading up to the midterm election, markets tend to experience
greater swings as investors navigate uncertainty surrounding the policy path for the
remainder of the presidential cycle (Exhibit 2A). Since 1930, the S&P 500 has averaged an
intrayear drawdown of 20% in midterm years, with the latest election in 2022 experiencing
a 25% intrayear pullback. This year, however, the S&P 500 largest peak-to-trough drawdown

has been 9%, considerably shallower than its long-term average (Exhibit 2B). Yet while
volatility may rise as election day approaches, 2026 has increasingly defied the traditional
midterm year playbook.
Why 2026 is Different. Despite entering a challenging stretch of the presidential cycle,
U.S. Equities have materially outpaced the typical year two pattern (Exhibit 2C). The S&P
500 experienced its best Q2 in a midterm year since 1938, surging 14.9% compared to an

average decline of 1.6%. From a monthly perspective, September stands out as the weakest
month, though month-to-date performance points to an unusual 1.0% rise. That resilience
follows a 2.6% gain in August, another month in which performance meaningfully exceeded
the historical decline of 0.5% (Exhibit 2D).
Through September, U.S. Equity resilience has also extended beyond headline returns with
both the frequency and magnitude of market pullbacks remaining relatively subdued. Market

corrections of 10% or more have occurred in just 29% of midterm election years since 1930.4
Against this backdrop, the market’s last correction came in spring 2025, while this year has
2 Bloomberg, Bureau of Labor Statistics. Data through August 31, 2026.
3 U.S. Treasury, Bureau of Economic Analysis. Data as of September 21, 2026.
4 Bloomberg, data through December 31, 2022.
Investment Implications
In our view, any related market
weakness could represent a

potential buying opportunity for
long-term investors.

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 28, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
seen only one 5%+ pullback compared to the average three per year, underscoring relatively
resilient market conditions.5 Year-to-date, the S&P 500 has increased 13%, well above the
average annual return of 3% according to Bloomberg through September 25, and on pace
to mark the strongest midterm election year since 2006. Taken together, 2026 has thus far
defied the traditionally weak midterm year seasonal pattern, reinforcing that every election

cycle is unique and historical trends may be a useful guide to navigate market volatility.
What’s Ahead? Forward performance is increasingly constructive for Equities. In the
post-war period, the S&P 500 rose 100% of the time in the six and 12 months following
elections, averaging 13% and 14%, respectively. 6 Even narrowing the time frame to three
months, markets ended higher 90% of the time. As investors push through the finish line,
the last three months of the year tend to be the strongest with October standing out,

ending higher 67% of the time. On average, Q4 has gained 5.6% since 1930, far surpassing
the preceding three quarters of election years. 7 Greater clarity about the U.S. fiscal policy
path serves as a potential tailwind for investors following the election.
To Take Home. Volatility tends to rise during midterm years, but history suggests that
reacting to short-term political uncertainty has rarely rewarded long-term investors. Over
longer horizons, the data suggests Equity returns and economic strength are shaped less

by election outcomes than by corporate profits, valuations, labor market conditions and
capital investments.

THE TICKER TAPE
What is the bond market telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
We believe the bond market is telling us that the Fed is on track to meeting their dual
mandate of price stability and full employment. More importantly, when examining the
full yield curve the 2-year Treasury yield is currently around 4.88%, which typically is
the most sensitive to the fed funds rate and tends to price in future potential policy

adjustments. At the same time, the 10-year Treasury yield is currently about 5.11%. This
leads us to believe that the market expects the Fed to engineer a couple of more hikes
while the back end of the curve is indicating that economic growth is still healthy.
Taken together, it appears the full curve, which has clearly flattened, believes the Fed is
doing its job, while supply shocks, for obvious reasons, are not leading to higher long-run
inflation. The backup in long-dated yields is growth, not necessarily inflation as indicated

by rising “real” yields. Therefore, we view the drift higher in yields as a more normal sign
than what many have originally suggested. We continue to emphasize higher economic
growth for longer, rising productivity in the medium term, supply shortages to remain in
key areas within the AI space, and shocks in the energy equation. We expect the energy
shocks to ultimately fade away over time, but we do not believe that growth fades
anytime soon. In our view, a more normal bond market pricing in Fed moves at the short

end, and growth at the long end helps financial conditions and economic growth operate
in a more durable manner.
In summary, the front-end of the curve expects 3 to 4 more hikes in the next 18 months
or so. This can be debated obviously based on future data especially short-term energy
supply shocks. Long-end yields are drifting higher based on five main factors below, in
our view.
1. Strong economic growth (both
services and manufacturing)
2. Hawkish Fed

3. Debt and deficit concerns
4. Lower demand for long duration
sovereign debt in general
5. Energy supply shocks
The largest weight being strong economic activity which kick starts portfolio
repositioning particularly as the Fed hikes rates. The repositioning stops once demand
rises again when yields reach attractive levels for the more patient longer term
investment community. The supply shocks ultimately end and debt and deficit worries will
likely remain, but they still take a backseat for now. Longer duration government debt is

becoming more attractive each week even in the face of the concerns above. Be patient

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

S&P 500 Index Price Return is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States. A price return index is a stock
market index that tracks only the price changes and capital gains or losses of its component securities, without including dividends or cash distributions.
S&P Global Flash U.S. Composite Purchasing Managers’ Index is an early monthly economic indicator that estimates overall private-sector business activity by combining manufacturing

prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,

US Treasuries have calmed. But plenty of opportunity and time
for markets to (re-)change their tune. Italian bond investors
signalled their liking of an electoral reform that would
potentially improve governability. We draw the parallel with
France, where improved governability is a prerequisite for a
material tightening of spreads

Padhraic Garvey, CFA and Benjamin Schroeder
With the UST 10yr yield dipping below 5.25%, it does not mean the bond
market sell-oﬀ is over, but rather on pause
Treasuries sail calmer waters, but we'd not
get too comfortable
It's been a good week for US bond auctions. Solid 10yr and 30yr
auctions over consecutive days show there is a level where buyers
step in. The 30yr auction saw a strong international and domestic
bid that took down the paper at secondary market levels, and the
previous day's 10yr auction came a tad rich to secondary. The 10yr
yield is back below 5.25%, partly a reﬂection of this, but also
reﬂective of some value hunters generally. It does not mean the

bond market sell-oﬀ is over, but it does at least put it on pause, at
least just for now.
We also note that Treasuries are holding on to the moderate
richness built since the increased long-end-buyback
announcement, in the guise of tighter swap spreads; the antithesis
of what we saw in France over the past weeks, where swap spreads
gapped wider by a factor of at least two. Nothing like that has
occurred in the US, as the 10yr yield holds at just under a 40bp

spread to 10yr SOFR.
That said, the 10yr swap spread is oﬀ its recent lows, and we
continue to anticipate a likely widening in US swap spreads ahead
as we head into the mid-terms with not nearly enough chat on
ﬁscal deﬁcit-reduction actions plans. Plenty of opportunity for
markets to wake up and decide to start to price that through a
widening in the implied Treasury credit spread to the SOFR risk-free
rate.
Italy highlights the importance of
governability

Thursday saw a recovery in European government bond spreads
that was led by Italian bonds. Their 10y spread over Bunds
tightened around 5bp towards 110bp. This is still above the levels
seen before French repricing started to spill over, but it came with a
backdrop of rising energy prices. Since February, higher oil prices
have tended to generally widen spreads and in particular hurt
Italian bonds relatively more.
The outperformance was attributed to the Italian PM Meloni

securing the ﬁnal vote for her electoral reform that would grant any
leading coalition with more than 42% of the votes bonus seats to
secure stable majorities. It is actually unclear whether she will
beneﬁt from this, but the prospect of clearer majorities and better
governability is in general a positive for anyone looking for
exposure to sovereign risk.
It also highlights what we see as a precondition for any lasting
recovery in French bond spreads – political predictability and

It will be another relatively quiet day in terms of macro

米長期金利５％超は警戒水準 
米国の10 年国債利回りが５％を超えた。これは単なる心理的な
節目ではなく、実体経済・株式市場の双方にとって警戒すべき水
準だ。図表１でマクロ環境を確認すると、長期金利は米国経済
の巡航速度とされる名目潜在成長率を上回っている。投資の期
待リターンよりも資金調達コストの方が高くなれば、実物資産投
資は抑制されやすい。住宅市場など、一部で金融環境の引き締
まりが鮮明化しており、これが景気悪化につながるリスクが高ま
っている。

図表１：米国の長期金利は名目潜在成長率を超過

注：シャドーは景気後退局面、潜在成長率は議会予算局推計  
出所：米議会予算局、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

株式市場への影響も無視できない。図表２の通り、長期金利は
Ｓ＆Ｐ５００指数の株式益利回りに肉薄している。無リスクの国債
で株式並みの利回りを確保できる状況では、投資家がリスクを
取る合理性が薄れるため、株価にはバリュエーション調整圧力
が掛かりやすい。今後さらに長期金利が上昇すれば、実物投資
から貯蓄へ、株式市場から債券市場へと資金シフトが進み、景
気減速と株安を招き得る。

図表２：イールドスプレッドの消滅で、株式に割高感

注：株式益利回り＝一株当たり純利益／株価  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

長期金利＝期待政策金利＋ﾀｰﾑﾌﾟﾚﾐｱﾑ 
図表３は米長期金利を、ＦＲＢの金融政策見通しを表す「期待政
策金利」と、財政リスクや国債需給を反映する「タームプレミアム」
に分解したものである。

図表３：長期金利＝期待政策金利＋タームプレミアム

注：期待政策金利とタームプレミアムはNY連銀のACMモデルの推計値  
出所：NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG 
 米長期金利が５％を突破。長期金利は名目潜在成長率を上回り、住宅市場などの一部で金融環境の引き
締まりがみられる。株式益利回りにも肉薄しており、景気減速と株安を警戒すべき水準  
 米金利上昇の背景はインフレと財政懸念の高まり。当面の米 10 年金利は 4.5～5.5％での推移を見込む  
 中間選挙前のポピュリズム的な財政政策は、インフレ・財政不安・金利上昇の悪循環を招く  
 市場の吸収力にも懸念。ＦＲＢの資産圧縮、海外中銀の米債売却、ＡＩ企業との資金争奪戦、インフレ懸念  
 米 30 年金利は財政リスクによる上振れで、当面は 4.9～5.9％での推移を予想。米金利の上昇は日本の超
長期金利にも波及する公算 
KEY 
POINT

（経済政策不確実性指数）
（年/月）
タームプレミアム
（右軸）
経済政策不確実性指数
（左軸）
（タームプレミアム、％）
イラン戦争以降の金利上昇は、原油高を背景としたインフレ懸
念と利上げ観測の高まりが主因であることがわかる。ただし、ト
ランプ政権発足後に高まったタームプレミアムも高水準を維持し
ており、現在の高金利は金融政策要因だけでは説明できない。

タームプレミアム： 
関心はトランプリスクから財政・需給懸念へ 
図表４の通り、トランプ政権発足後のタームプレミアムは、経済
政策不確実性指数と概ね連動してきた。関税、移民、拡張財政、
中銀への政治圧力、地政学リスクなど、トランプ政権における政
策の予見可能性の低下が、ドル資産への信認を損ねたことが
背景である。  
しかし足元では両者の関係に変化がみられる。これは市場の関
心がトランプリスクから、財政悪化や国債需給懸念へ移行した
可能性を示唆するものだ。

図表４：タームプレミアムはトランプリスクと共に動いてきた

注：経済政策不確実性指数は、政治・経済、財政・金融政策、外交、安保、地政学等
の先行き不透明感を表す指数  
出所：EPU、NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

財政規律の緩みと「トランプ配当」 
もっとも、財政懸念は今に始まった話ではない。図表５は米財政
赤字のＧＤＰ比と失業率（上下反転）の関係を示したものである。
かつては、失業率が悪化すれば財政のアクセルを踏み、改善
すればブレーキを踏むという一定の規律があった。しかし、トラ
ンプ政権第一期が始動した 2017 年頃から、完全雇用にもかか
わらず財政赤字が拡大しており、放漫財政が常態化している。  
では、なぜ今になって財政問題に焦点が当たるのか。背景には、
中間選挙が迫るなかで加速するトランプ政権のポピュリズム政
策がある。政権支持率はインフレを理由に過去最低水準に落ち
込んでいる。そこでトランプ大統領は、共和党が中間選挙で上
下両院を制した場合、有権者一人当たり5,000ドルの「トランプ配
当」を支給する方針を打ち出した。1.2 兆ドル規模の歳出拡大の
財源をどう賄うのか、それが財政収支と国債需給にどう影響す
るのかが不安視されている。

図表５：トランプ第一期政権以降、財政規律が緩み、 
好況下での財政赤字拡大が常態化

出所：BLS、BEA、ソニーFG

これは悪循環の入口である。インフレによる国民の不満に財政
政策で応じれば、需要は過熱し、インフレが加速する。そのイン
フレが新たな不満を生み、さらなる財政出動を招けば国債発行
が増え、長期金利が上昇する。また、利払い費の増加は財政赤
字をさらに拡大させる。結果として、インフレ、財政不安、長期金
利上昇が相互に増幅し合う負の連鎖に陥りかねない。最終的に
財政規律を迫るのは債券市場となり、金利上昇が経済・金融市
場に深刻な打撃を与えて初めて財政再建が進む展開も十分に
想定される。

国債市場の問題は「供給量」より「吸収力」 
現在、市場の焦点は、国債発行額の増加よりも、その吸収力（国
債需要）の低下に移りつつある。要因は４つある。

第１に、ＦＲＢの資産圧縮の進展である。これは2022 年以降の
動きだが、ウォーシュＦＲＢ議長がＱＴに前向きであることで、あ
らためて警戒を呼んでいる。  
第２に、ウクライナ戦争後の傾向として、新興国を中心に「ドル離
れ」が進み、各国の中央銀行が外貨準備として保有する米国債
を売却して金の準備を積み増す動きが続いている。  
第３に、ＡＩ関連企業の大量起債がある。データセンターなどの設
備投資の資金を得るため、大規模な社債発行が行われており、
資本の争奪戦を招いている。  
第４に、中東情勢に伴う原油高がインフレやインフレ期待を高め、
債券投資家を慎重にさせている。

特に第１の要因は次頁図表６に明瞭に表れている。ＦＲＢの国債
保有比率とタームプレミアムには逆相関がみられ、ＦＲＢが資産
圧縮を進めるほど、国債を引き受ける民間投資家がより高い上
乗せ金利を要求する姿である。今後ＱＴが加速すれば、ターム
プレミアムにはさらなる上昇圧力がかかるだろう。

イラン攻撃
図表６：ＦＲＢの資産圧縮がタームプレミアムを押し上げ

注：米国債保有比率＝FRBの米国債保有額／米国債残高  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ベッセント財務長官は長期債のバイバック拡充で流動性の改善
を図っているが、効果は限定的だ。バイバックは市場機能を支
えても、財政赤字や国債残高を減らさないためである。財政や
需給の根本的な改善が見込めない以上、当面のタームプレミア
ムは 0.6～0.9％の高水準で推移しやすい。  
なお、各種世論調査では中間選挙で共和党が上下両院を失うと
の見方が高まっている。その場合、①新議会発足前にトランプ
政権が対イラン攻撃を強化する可能性、②新議会・民主党が主
導する財政拡張の加速、③関税や反移民政策など大統領令に
よる政策の一段の先鋭化、④ＦＲＢへの政治圧力の強化などが
想定される。インフレや財政悪化、中銀の独立性への懸念を高
め、タームプレミアムの押し上げにつながる可能性がある。

期待政策金利は利上げを織り込み上昇 
一方、期待政策金利は経済指標の強弱に敏感に反応している。
図表７のエコノミック・サプライズ指数は経済指標の予想と実績
の差を示すが、イラン攻撃以降はインフレ指標の上振れによっ
て指数が上昇し、それとともに期待政策金利も切り上がった。ま
た、直近では９月ＰＭＩの予想以上の強さも利上げ期待を高めて
いる。

図表７：期待政策金利は経済指標の強弱に反応

注：サプライズ指数は経済指標の市場予想と実績の乖離。ここでは雇用と物価に関
するサプライズ指数の合計値を用いた  
出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG 
期待政策金利は先々の政策金利の予想値であるため、市場の
利上げ観測に沿って動く。図表８の通り、市場は年内２回以上の
利上げを織り込んでおり、数年後の政策金利は５％を突破する
との見立てである。一方、市場の予想は先走りしやすく、大きな
揺り戻しも生じやすい。そうした点を考慮すると、向こう数年の期
待政策金利は3.9％～4.6％での推移となろう。

図表８：期待政策金利は将来の政策金利を織り込む

出所：NY連銀、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

以上を総合すると、長期金利＝期待政策金利（3.9～4.6％）＋タ
ームプレミアム（0.6～0.9％）となり、当面は4.5～5.5％のレンジで
の推移が見込まれる。ただし、冒頭で見たように、現在の長期
金利はすでに米国経済・株式市場に打撃を与え得る水準に達し
ている。原油高や国債需給懸念などにより、景気拡大を背景とし
ない「悪い金利上昇」がさらに進めば、景気悪化と株安が顕在化
するリスクは一段と高まろう。

超長期金利が映す財政不安 
図表９の通り、米国の30 年金利と10 年金利は密に連動してい
るが、両者のスプレッドは不況期に拡大し、好況期に縮小する
傾向がある。不況期は将来の利下げ観測から短中期ゾーンの
金利が大きく低下する一方、超長期ゾーンは動きにくいためだ。

図表９：米30 年-10 年金利は好況期に一致、不況期に乖離

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

（海外要因、左軸）
（年/月）

日本10年金利
（国内要因、右軸）
（％） （％）
ところがトランプ政権発足以降（2025 年～）は、堅調な景気が続
いたにもかかわらずスプレッドが拡大しており、景気循環では説
明できない。財政赤字と国債需給への懸念が、30 年金利に上乗
せされているとみるべきである。

図表10 は30 年-10 年金利スプレッドと景気（ＧＤＰギャップ）の関
係を見たものである。過去は両者が密接に連動しており、超長
期ゾーンのイールドカーブの傾きは景気次第で動いていたこと
が改めて確認できる。しかし足元ではこの関係に明確な乖離が
生じており、景気では説明できない上乗せ分＝財政プレミアム
の存在を裏づけている。

図表10：ＧＤＰギャップと30 年－10 年金利スプレッド

注：GDPギャップの予想は米議会予算局による 
出所：米議会予算局、Bloomberg、ソニーFG

ＧＤＰギャップから見れば妥当なスプレッドは0.2％ｐｔ程度だが、
一時はこれが0.7％ｐｔまで拡大し、足元でも 0.4％ｐｔ程度となって
いる。当面はこの財政プレミアムが残り、30 年金利は10 年金利
＋0.4％ｐｔ程度で推移すると見込まれる。10 年金利の予想レンジ
に基づけば、30 年金利は4.9～5.9％となる。中間選挙に向けて
財政不安が強まれば、景気が減速しても超長期金利が上昇し続
けるリスクがある。

米国の財政問題は日本の金利に波及 
米国の超長期金利の上昇は日本にも波及する。図表 11 の通り、
日本の40 年金利は、日本の10 年金利に内包される「国内要因」
と、米国30 年金利に反映される「海外要因」の双方によって押し
上げられてきた。  
国内要因としては、日銀の独立性を巡るビハインド・ザ・カーブ
懸念や、高市政権の積極財政に対する信認問題が金利上昇要
因となってきた。ただし、７月末の日米協調介入以降は、米国か
ら政策修正を求める圧力が強まった。８月末のＧ20 ではベッセ
ント財務長官から「リフレ政策をやめるべき」との要求もあった。
こうした外圧は、日銀の利上げを後押しする一方、高市政権の
財政拡大路線には一定の歯止めとなろう。

図表11： 40 年金利は国内要因と海外要因が押し上げ

出所：Bloomberg、ソニーFG

今後、日銀の利上げペースが加速する可能性はあるが、長期
金利の水準を左右するのは利上げの最終到達点である。市場
が予想するターミナルレート（OIS２年先１か月）は既に日銀の中
立金利レンジ（1.1～2.5％）の上限を突破していることから、期待
政策金利の上昇余地は大きくないと思われる。このため、国内
要因による超長期金利への上昇圧力は徐々に弱まっていくと考
えられる（高市政権の政策スタンスの先鋭化や日銀が中立金利
を上回る引き締めを行うなどの不確実性は残る）。  
一方、海外要因の影響は強まりそうだ。米30年金利が本稿の予
想通り4.9～5.9％で推移すれば、日本の40 年金利は3.8～4.9％
程度で推移する可能性が高い（※）。もっとも、このレンジは米30
年金利のみを説明変数とする推計に基づくため、日本の10 年
金利に反映される国内要因によって上下に振れる余地がある。  
現在、日本の超長期金利を左右しているのは、国内の金融・財
政政策と米国の財政・国債需給だが、重点は後者へ移りつつあ
る。今後は日銀や国内景気に加えて、米国の30 年債入札やＱＴ、
財政政策が日本の超長期債市場を占う上で重要性を増すので
はないか。

渡辺 浩志 
※）米 30 年金利と日本の 40 年金利の間の直接的な裁定関係は薄いが、両市 
場はグローバル投資家による相対価値取引やポートフォリオ調整を通じて 
連動している。

Document 322: Sukuk_ Structure, stability and strategic opportunity (for Private Bank only)

### Page 1

Sukuk: Structure,  
stability and strategic 
opportunity

September 2026

### Page 2

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  2

Steve Brice 
Global Chief Investment Officer

Manpreet Gill 
Chief Investment Officer, EMEA

Hannah Chew 
Portfolio Strategist

Islamic finance is growing at a rapid pace, driven 
primarily by increasing demand for investors who 
seek Shariah -compliant investments. Sukuk, often 
referred to as Islamic bonds, surpassed USD 1trn in 
2025, making them one of the fastest -growing 
segments o f Islamic finance. The asset class’s 
unique value proposition is driving its popularity, 
especially in an increasingly volatile global  
economic landscape. 
In this publication, we will explore the evolving role of Sukuk in wealth management, 
examining market fundamentals, risk frameworks and emerging opportunities within 
this market.

### Page 3

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  3

Sukuk, commonly referred to as Islamic bonds, are Shariah-compliant investment 
certificates representing ownership interests in underlying assets, usufructs (the 
right to use an asset and receive its benefits, without owning it) or ventures. In 
return, Sukuk holders receive periodic distributions, typically in the form of rental 
payments (Ijarah), profit-sharing (Mudarabah) or asset-generated cash flows, and 
upon maturity the principal amount is repaid. Sukuk differ from conventional 
bonds in several ways: 
Fig. 1  
Sukuk vs. conventional bonds

Aspect  Conventional bonds Sukuk

Core principle  Lending (time-value of money) Asset ownership 
Underlying asset  Any asset, business or project Must be Sharia-compliant 
Returns  Guaranteed (fixed or floating interest) Not legally guaranteed (depends on 
underlying asset’s performance) 
Risk   Issuer bears all performance risk Shared risk between parties 
Transparency  Moderate (depends on disclosure) High (asset identification required)

Source: Standard Chartered

The role of Sukuk in the investing landscape is evolving amid 
global uncertainty and rising geopolitical tensions. Investors 
are increasingly seeking more stable and asset ‑backed 
alternatives. An investor can consider investing in Sukuk for 
the following reasons: 
Resilient 
Sukuk provide asset -backed collateral that conventional 
bonds often lack, offering a cushion during volatile market 
conditions. Historically, Islamic investments, including Sukuk’, 
have been countercyclical, i.e. they tend to expand or remain 
stable when c onventional markets contract. Across major 
market stress from the 2018 US-China Trade War through the 
2026 Middle East Conflict, Sukuk bonds consistently 
outperformed Global Bonds — delivering smaller drawdowns 
in risk-off episodes such as the 2018 US-China trade war and 
2022 higher for longer inflation, and greater resilience in 
geopolitical oil shocks, underscoring the asset class's 
structural defensive characteristics.

1 The evolving role of Sukuk

Strong fundamentals
The Sukuk market experienced an unprecedented 25% growth in issuance in 2025, with issuance nearly USD 300bn. According
to some  reports,  the  Sukuk  market  could  reach  USD  3trn  by  2030  driven  by  increased  demand  for  Shariah-compliant
investments, sovereign funds’ diversification needs, development of regulatory frameworks for Sukuk and growth in Middle East

capital markets.  Nearly  80%  of  Fitch ‑rated Sukuk  carry  investment ‑grade ratings  and  have  little  or  no  history  of  default,
underscoring the sector’s strong fundamentals. These factors should help to instil confidence among investors, who can look at
Sukuk as central building blocks of their portfolio.

Convergence with sustainable investing trends
Sukuk and sustainable bonds share more common ground than their different origins suggest, making sustainable Sukuk a
natural bridge between Islamic finance and sustainable investing. This also helps explain Sukuk’s growing appeal beyond
traditional Muslim investors, as evidenced by Sustainable Sukuk issuance rising from USD 464mn in 2017 to USD 9bn in 2024,

representing a CAGR of 52.7%. The overlap starts with what Sukuk exclude: Shariah principles prohibit interest, excessive
uncertainty and speculation, and screen out sectors financing harmful industries such as tobacco, gambling, weapons, adult
entertainment and alcohol – a negative screen that closely aligns with the exclusionary (negative screening) method, commonly
used in sustainable investing. Tradi tional Sukuk bonds require proceeds to be linked to identifiable real assets or projects,

creating a ring-fenced structure like use-of-proceeds sustainable bonds. Combined with Shariah’s broader objectives of social
welfare, resource stewardship and equita ble risk-sharing, this creates strong alignment with the United Nations Sustainable
Development Goals.
Green Sukuk is an established example, where proceeds are earmarked for environmental projects such as renewable energy,
clean transport, energy-efficient buildings or sustainable water and wastewater management. Issuers must meet both Shariah

approval from a recognised scholarly board and alignment with International Capital Market Association (ICMA) Green Bond
Principles.
Traditional Sukuk already comply with Shariah laws such as avoiding Riba (interest -based returns arising from lending
transactions), Gharar (investments with ambiguity) and Maysir (speculation). Sustainable Sukuk build on this foundation by
adding environmental and social objectives. The table below highlights how sustainable Sukuk differ from traditional Sukuk.

Fig. 4

Digital integration
The integration  of  blockchain  technology  and  digital  platforms  is  revolutionising  Sukuk  issuance  and  trading,  dramatically
reducing transaction costs and expanding accessibility to  a broader  investor base.  For instance, SAKKEX (an  independent
blockchain-based Sukuk marketplace) streamlines drafting, issuance, and near ‑instant settlement. Its Sukuk Wallet App also
allows investors to trade on the go. Several such digital platforms now allow tokenised Sukuk that enable fractional ownership,

automated Shariah  compliance  verification  and  real-time  settlement,  thereby  addressing  challenges  that  have  persisted  in
Islamic finance markets. Digital tools also simplify cross border Sukuk transactions by enabling end to end digital origination and
trading, with smart contracts automating profit sharing to cut operational costs and enhance transparency.

### Page 6

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026 6
Sukuk structures define the nature of ownership, risk -sharing and return
generation. Several types of Sukuk structures exist and are defined by the
underlying Shariah contract. We discuss some prominent ones below and see
how they align with various phases of an investor’s wealth journey:
Sukuk al-Ijarah is a Shariah-compliant lease-based bond where the investor gains partial ownership in a physical

asset such as a building, airport or equipment. The asset is then leased back to the issuer who pays a nominal
(pre-agreed) rent to the investor. An investor seeking stable, predictable income streams with lower volatility can
consider investing in these Sukuk. The original principal amount is returned at the end of the lease period.
Ijarah Sukuk are ideal for the wealth preservation phase of an investor’s wealth journey. For instance, investors
nearing retirement typically need to restructure their portfolios by shifting from growth to preservation while

generating steady income to cover living expenses. Ijarah Sukuk deliver on both fronts: protecting capital through
asset-backed structures while providing predictable rental income that supports household needs without
depleting principal.
Sukuk al-Murabahah are cost-plus financing structures where the issuer buys an asset and immediately resells
it to the investors at a marked-up price, with payment deferred over a pre-determined time. Consider this: Let us

assume, a company needs USD 100m. A special purpose vehicle (SPV), established by the company, mobilises
funds from investors and buys commodities worth USD 100m and immediately resells them to the company for
USD 112m payable over time. The USD 12m difference is the agreed profit margin. Murabahah Sukuk ar e best
suited for investors seeking income over a short to medium term, typically, 1-5 years. Investments in this type of
Sukuk return the principal gradually throughout the investment period rather than only at maturity.

Investments in Murabahah Sukuk are best suited for wealth protection and income generation. Unlike Ijarah which
return principal as a lump sum at maturity, Murabahah amortise both profit and principal in each instalment. This
makes them ideal for retirees who need to systematically draw down capital to fund living expenses without
depleting their portfolio too quickly.
Sukuk al-Mudarabah is a profit-sharing partnership arrangement where investors provide 100% of the capital

and operate as silent partners. The issuer manages the business or project as the active partner. Under a
Mudarabah arrangement, financial losses arising from normal bu siness risks are borne by the investors in
proportion to their capital contribution, while the Mudarib loses its effort and entitlement to profit. However, where
losses result from negligence, misconduct, breach of agreed terms, or misconduct by the Mudari b, the Mudarib
may be held liable. Think of it as venture capital meets bonds; you fund the business, professionals run it and

you share the profits or bear the losses resulting from the business activities. Mudarabah Sukuk are often
regarded as one of the  structures most closely aligned with the Shariah principle of profit and loss sharing.
However, they bear higher risk and lower predictability compared to other structures.
Investors with a growth mindset and open to high -risk tolerance can consider investing in these Sukuk . They
best align with accumulation and growth phase of the wealth journey, typically, when investors have long time

horizons to absorb volatility and can afford capital risk for higher returns. However, to balance risk, it is important
for investors to diversify across various types of Sukuk. Even within Mudarabah, they can consider investing
in multiple projects, sectors and geographies.
1 Sukuk structures and
wealth mandates

### Page 7

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  7

A joint venture partnership structure where both investors and the issuer contribute capital is Sukuk al -
Musharakah. In this structure, both parties (issuer and investors) jointly provide capital, participate in managing 
the venture, and share profits or losses based on agreed upon ratios. This structure appeals to investors focused 
on growth, interested in active participation, and prepared to take on greater risk in pursuit of higher returns. 
Musharakah Sukuk are best suited for the growth and accumulation phase where investors are building wealth 
aggressively, comfortable with high risk and able to lock up capital for the long term (typically 5+ years).

Sukuk al-Wakalah is an agency arrangement where investors appoint a representative from the issuing company 
as their Wakil (agent) to invest their funds in specific Shariah-compliant assets or projects in exchange for a fee. 
This arrangement is similar to a mutual fund or ETF, where the agent manages a diversified pool of Shariah 
compliant assets on behalf of the investors. Investors benefit from professional fund management, diversification, 
transparency and flexibility. Depending on issuer preferences, Wakalah Sukuk can b e structured with lower 
investment thresholds, making them accessible to a wide range of investors. 
Wakalah Sukuk suitability depends heavily on the underlying asset allocation and risk profile. Conservative 
Wakalah structures investing in stable, income -generating assets suit wealth preservation. Moderate -risk 
Wakalah can complement accumulation portfolios as a diversification tool, though not as a primary growth driver 
as their structure and risk mandate limit upside potential.

Sukuk al-Istisna is a project financing structure based on a manufacturing or construction contract. Investors 
provide upfront capital to finance the construction of specific assets. Upon completion, the asset is sold to an end 
buyer (often the original issuer or a third party) who makes deferred payments including the cost plus a mark-up, 
which are then distributed to investors. Istisna suit seasoned investors with construction finance expertise and 
those who can absorb development risk and commit capital for the long term. Owing to its complexity and higher 
risk profile, this type of Sukuk typically requires large initial investments, making it less accessible to retail 
investors.  
In the wealth cycle, Istisna are best aligned with the accumulation and growth phase, when long time horizons 
can absorb construction delays and development risks. The long-term commitment and potential for cost overruns 
or project failure make it unsuitable for later wealth stages such as retirement.

Sukuk al -Salam is an Islamic bond backed by a forward purchase contract where investors pay upfront for 
commodities to be delivered later. At its core, they are similar to forward contracts. However, unlike a forward 
contract, Salam contracts are fully prepaid, standardi sed and designed with clearly defined delivery terms to 
reduce uncertainty (gharar). Under this arrangement, an investor profits from the difference between the lower 
Salam purchase price (paid today) and the higher resale price (received at delivery).  
Salam Sukuk carry high delivery and price risk and aligns well with investors in the growth and accumulation 
phase. Salam requires strong commodity‑market expertise, deep understanding of production and delivery risks, 
and the ability to assess the issuer’s operational capacity to deliver the specified goods on time.

Owing to their ethical framework and asset -backed nature, Sukuk are often
considered relatively less risky than conventional bonds. However, they are not
immune to risks. Unique challenges such as asset quality deterioration and
structure-specific exposures require rigorous risk management.

Due diligence in Sukuk extends beyond traditional credit
analysis and an investor should also consider the following:
• Shariah compliance verification can be done by evaluating

the Sukuk structure and confirming that its mandates and
underlying assets fully align with Islamic principles.
• Evaluating asset quality by verifying tangibility, cash -
generating capability and legal ownership can help
investor reduce risk.
• Assessing creditworthiness and market depth will help in
identifying financially strong Sukuk issuers and ensuring
adequate liquidity.
• Cross-border Sukuk investments remain viable,
contingent on careful scrutiny of legal documentation

across multiple jurisdictions to ensure enforceability and
investor protection.
Robust due diligence and proactive risk mitigation strategies
are essential to protect capital and stay aligned with Islamic
financial principles.
1 Risk management and due diligence

### Page 10

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  10

Staying up to date on the latest developments in 
the Sukuk market is important as this space is 
constantly evolving and rapidly expanding. 
Moreover, Islamic finance is not static; Shariah 
interpretations related to Sukuk structures are 
periodically redefined. Staying informed protects 
investors from investing in non -compliant 
options. We look at some of the factors that are 
guiding the future of this landscape:

1 Emerging trends

Green Sukuk issuance is projected to grow materially through 
2030, reflecting the natural convergence of Islamic finance and 
sustainability principles. Key sectors include renewable 
energy, green buildings, low -carbon transportation, water and 
waste management and sustainable agriculture.

Tech advancements  are revolutionising Sukuk origination, 
trading and accessibility. Digitisation offers numerous benefits 
to the Islamic finance landscape: streamlined issuance and 
trading processes, enhanced accessibility through 
tokenisation, immutable audit trails via bl ockchain technology 
and improved secondary market liquidity.

A major regulatory shift is likely to create standardised rules 
across Islamic finance markets globally. The pivotal 
development is AAOIFI's Standard 62, launching in 2026, 
which mandates transparent, Shariah-compliant asset-backing 
and genuine asset ownership. This can fundament ally 
strengthen the Islamic authenticity of Sukuk structures.

The Sukuk market is rapidly expanding beyond its traditional 
Islamic finance strongholds. Non-traditional territories  in 
Africa (e.g., Algeria, Benin and Nigeria), Europe (e.g., 
Luxemburg, UK) and Asia (e.g., Turkey, Pakistan) are 
emerging as Islamic capital market gateways. This expansion 
underscores the evolution of Sukuk from a niche Islamic 
product to a globally reco gnised asset class attracting diverse 
sovereigns and investors across continents.

### Page 11

InvesTips: Sukuk: Structure, stability and strategic opportunity  |  September 2026  11

Conclusion

Ultimately, Sukuk demonstrate that Shariah compliance and sound investment 
strategy are not competing priorities, but complementary ones. By ensuring that 
portfolios are grounded in real economic activity by offering stability through 
tangible asset -backing and transparent structures that channel capital towards 
productive use. Their foundation in ethical, responsible financing appeals to 
investors seeking both resilience and purpose. As global markets increasingly 
prioritise integrity and long-term sustainability, Sukuk stand out as a compelling tool 
for Shariah -compliant investors who want meaningful diversification without 
compromising on their religious and ethical principles.

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

Monetary Authority of Singapore (MAS)

We expect another slight increase in the 
SGD NEER policy band slope at the MAS’ 
October review, with no change to the 
band’s width or centre. Import cost 
pressures and resilient growth favour a 
calibrated tightening, rather than a more 
forceful adjustment, in line with the 
global monetary policy stance.

Also due to be released alongside the 
MAS’ policy decision are the 3Q26 
advance GDP estimates, which we 
expect to show another quarter of strong 
growth at 5.4% yoy (1.4% qoq sa), 
compared with 5.9% yoy (1.4% qoq sa) in 
2Q26. Robust economic performance 
continued to be driven by artificial 
intelligence ( AI)-led trade activity, 
supporting the manufacturing and 
wholesale trade sectors, while strength 
in the financial sector underpinned the 
resilience of the modern services cluster. 
This came despite a moderation of the 
transport & storage sector, and 
weakness in consumer -facing food 
services and retail sectors.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect Malaysia’s advance GDP 
estimate for 3Q26 to register above -
trend growth of 5.0% yoy, albeit 
moderating from 6.0% yoy  in 2Q26. The 
overall expansion continued to be 
supported by ongoing strength in the 
export-oriented manufacturing sector, 
underpinned by strong  AI-related 
demand for electronics, and sustained 
domestic demand, backed by healthy 
investment and private consumption 
trends. However, contraction in mining 
and weak agricultural activity dragged 
overall growth.

Singapore

We expect firm Singapore non -oil 
domestic exports (NODX) growth of 28.0% 
yoy in September 2026, although 
moderating from August’s exceptional 
surge of 46.2%. Although favourable 
base effects largely faded in September, 
electronics shipments likely remained 
the primary growth driver, underpinned 
by robust AI -related demand amid 
sustained global investment in AI 
infrastructure, while non-electronics 
exports were uneven.

The Week Ahead October 9, 2026
